Measured values (the evidence run; candidate unless named; the same in both palettes unless named):
- **Thumb fill against the list surface** (`fill`: the modal colour of the thumb's inner 4 px (rest) or 6 px (hovered, pressed) columns against the dialog's canvas sampled in its left padding). At rest the thumb is `border` on canvas, (43, 55, 73) on (16, 21, 31) in Midnight and (203, 211, 228) on (246, 247, 252) in Porcelain; hovered or pressed it is `muted`, (164, 177, 197) and (77, 91, 120). The resting style is the existing review-dialog scrollbar's, unchanged by this task; the design reviewer rules on it. Floors set before the run: 1.3 at rest, 3.0 hovered or pressed.

| Thumb | Midnight | Porcelain | Frames |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| At rest | 1.519:1 | 1.404:1 | `top`, `focus`, `middle` and `end`, every list and size |
| Hovered | 8.419:1 | 6.366:1 | `thumb-hover`, every list and size |
| Pressed, dragged | 8.419:1 | 6.366:1 | `thumb-drag`, chooser, remotes and Tags, both sizes |
| Where the rows fit | 1.0:1 (none) | 1.0:1 (none) | `fits`, chooser, remotes and Tags |

  On the base the same regions read 1.0 (no thumb) except where the pointer hovered a row at the thumb's place (recorded only): the chooser and Tags at 18 pt 1.058 (Midnight) and 1.302 (Porcelain), and the Push to… list 1.159 and 1.210 at both sizes. These are a hovered row's or button's fill, not a thumb.
- **Thumb position follows the offset** (`glyph_contrast` ink boxes in the track column, window pixel rows inclusive; recorded). Each resting thumb is 6 px wide at x 743–748 (chooser), 763–768 (remotes), 773–778 (Tags) and 793–798 (Push to…), and 8 px wide (2 px further left) hovered or pressed:

| List | Size | Thumb length | At the top | Middle (share of travel) | At the end | Pressed, 100 px above the end |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Chooser | 13 pt | 90 px | 173–262 | 283–373 (0.47) | 405–494 | 304–393 |
| Chooser | 18 pt | 63 px | 232–294 | 358–421 (0.49) | 491–553 | 390–452 |
| Remotes | 13 pt | 102 px | 175–276 | 306–407 (0.52) | 425–526 | 324–425 |
| Remotes | 18 pt | 73 px | 235–307 | 367–439 (0.47) | 514–586 | 413–485 |
| Tags | 13 pt | 109 px | 174–282 | 297–405 (0.51) | 417–525 | 316–424 |
| Tags | 18 pt | 77 px | 206–282 | 349–425 (0.52) | 481–557 | 380–456 |
| Push to… | 13 pt | 56 px | 260–315 | 306–361 (0.48) | 356–411 | not pressed |
| Push to… | 18 pt | 40 px | 349–388 | 400–439 (0.46) | 461–500 | not pressed |

  Each thumb starts 4 px below its column's top at the top and ends 4 px above its bottom at the end (the kit's inset). Every pressed thumb lies 101 px above its end position for a 100 px pointer motion (all 12 candidate `thumb-drag` frames). The `middle-left-top` and `end-left-top` checks read 1.0 where the resting thumb was, and `thumb-drag-scrolled` found the rows moved under the drag (5,189–5,766 px changed in the chooser, 15,873–19,139 in Tags, 29,061–33,103 in remotes); on the base the list did not scroll (recorded: the pressed and the hovered row's fills and a tooltip in the chooser and Tags, nothing in remotes, where the press landed in a card's padding).
- **Focus rings** (`ring`, every side continuous with no gap and 2 px wide along its straight runs; at 18 pt the Push to… and Remove… rings read 1 px at their anti-aliased rounded corners, in the base too; contrast against the surface just outside): 11.43:1 in Midnight and 6.55:1 in Porcelain in every `focus`, `focus-hover`, `thumb-hover` and `end` frame of the four lists at both sizes. The candidate's first-control ring boxes: chooser `[244, 166, 737, 206]` (13 pt) and `[244, 225, 737, 278]` (18 pt); Tags `[214, 167, 767, 207]` and `[214, 199, 767, 252]`; Push to… `[194, 253, 787, 287]` and `[194, 342, 787, 387]`; the remote manager's first Remove… `[664, 196, 746, 230]` and `[631, 266, 743, 311]`. Measured on the committed base `focus` crops, not by the analysis, the base draws the same rings 19 px wider (x1 756, 786, 806) or, in the remote manager, 19 px further right (`[683, 196, 765, 230]`, `[650, 266, 762, 311]`). The analysis's base `focus-ring` results are recorded only and report the right side missing because their region follows the candidate's layout; they do not measure the base ring.
- **Clearance, none overlapping** (decision 4): "ring to thumb" is `clearance` from the ring's outermost right pixel, counting surface pixels along the control's middle row to the first non-surface pixel. "Ring to track" is derived. The track is canvas on the canvas dialog and cannot be seen, so its left edge comes from the resting thumb's measured right edge: the kit insets the thumb 4 px from the track's right edge, and the track is 16 px wide (`const THUMB_INSET: Pixels = px(4.);`, `const THUMB_ACTIVE_INSET: Pixels = px(4.);` and `const WIDTH: Pixels = px(4. * 2. + 8.);` in `vendor/gpui-base/src/scrollbar.rs`). For the chooser, the thumb ends at x 748, so the track's right edge is x 752 and its left edge 752 − 15 = x 737. The hovered thumb, 8 px wide with the same right edge, agrees.

| List | Size | Ring's last pixel | Track (derived) | Ring → track | Ring → thumb at rest | Ring → hovered thumb |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Chooser | 13, 18 pt | x 736 | x 737–752 | 0 px | 6 px | 4 px |
| Tags | 13, 18 pt | x 766 | x 767–782 | 0 px | 6 px | 4 px |
| Push to… | 13, 18 pt | x 786 | x 787–802 | 0 px | 6 px | 4 px |
| Remotes | 13 pt | x 745 | x 757–772 | 11 px | 7 px to the card's border (x 753), then 9 px | 7, then 7 px |
| Remotes | 18 pt | x 742 | x 757–772 | 14 px | 10 px to the card's border (x 753), then 9 px | 10, then 7 px |

  The ring ends where the track begins, as the gutter is built (16 px track plus the ring's 3 px of room), and its right side is continuous in every frame (2 px along its straight run), so the track covers no ring pixel. In the remote manager the card's border lies between the ring and the track, 3 px from the track. The resting values hold at the list's end (`end-clearance`) and with the control hovered (`focus-hover-clearance`); with the thumb hovered (`thumb-hover-clearance`) they are the last column's.
- **Unchanged outside the lists:** base against candidate, 0 differing pixels outside the list's bounds in every `top` crop (`top-only-the-list`, every variant and list). In the chooser, whose rows are left-aligned and transparent at rest, the `top` crops differ only in the thumb (`top-only-the-thumb`: 0 px outside the gutter). Recorded only: Tags and Push to… centre their labels, so the labels move with the narrower rows (12,370–13,847 and 3,345–4,358 px inside the lists), and the remote manager's cards end 19 px further left (3,688–3,732 px).
- **A list whose rows fit draws as the base** (`fits-same-as-base`, base against candidate, the whole crop, no mask): 0 px for the chooser filtered to `9` (topic-09, topic-19, topic-29), the remote manager filtered to `2` (mirror-02, mirror-12) and Tags filtered to `v1.01`, at both sizes and in both palettes. The Push to… list cannot fit in this fixture; `push_list_that_fits_paints_no_thumb` covers it.
- **Latency** (first changed frame after each key, every launch's `flow-log.json`, single observations, not a timing claim): Tab 28–72 ms on the base and 28–70 ms on the candidate (median 33 ms each, n = 588 each); Up, Down, Return and Escape 10–73 ms on the base and 7–68 ms on the candidate. BackSpace, which brings an overflowing list back after the filter, 30–52 ms on the base (median 32.5, n = 8) and 34–68 ms on the candidate (median 49.5, n = 8), consistent with the candidate drawing once more to add the gutter.

`qa.py scenario run` scanned the 184 crops in `commit/` with the local template set (28 templates) on their exact bytes: no match (114.02 s). The coordinator scanned the 184 crops again as copied into the evidence commit's tree, with the same 28 templates (`qa.py privacy scan --templates .local/privacy/templates --redacted`, 02:22 to 02:24 UTC): no match. A text search of this entry and the committed `scenario.json` for the owner's name, e-mail address, home directory and host name found nothing. The crops show only the fixture's branch, tag and remote names, its `$EVIDENCE/fixtures/tab-lists-scroll-cues/remotes/…` paths, short object IDs and commit subjects, and the app's labels; eight committed crops were viewed at full size (the 18 pt Tags and remote-manager `fits`, the 13 pt chooser `fits`, the 13 pt remote-manager `thumb-drag`, the 18 pt chooser `middle`, the 13 pt Tags `thumb-drag`, the 18 pt Push to… `focus-hover` and the base's 18 pt Push to… `end`), along with the rehearsals' full-window frames.

Findings and limitations:
- **Return in the filtered Tags dialog closes it through Done** instead of inspecting the focused row (rehearsal 3, candidate). The route inspects with a click instead. The base was not checked, and the change does not touch the dialog's key handling. **Ruling (coordinator, 02:27 UTC, on the design review):** not a defect of this change but a follow-up. The kit binds `enter` to `Confirm` in the dialog's own key context (`vendor/gpui-base/src/dialog.rs:91`), and the candidate only wraps each list in a plain element with no key context or listener, so the key route is the base's (inferred from the code, not observed on the base).
- **The pressed thumb moved 101 px for a 100 px pointer motion** in all 12 candidate drag frames; recorded, not a finding of the criterion.
- **The Push to… list** is hovered only (decision 3), and its fits case is the view test's (decision 2).
- **The middle and end are reached by Tab**, so a focused row shows there (decision 7). A wheel step over a list can reach the dialog body too, since GPUI hands the delta to every scrollable element under the pointer, so a wheel-only middle could have moved the dialog. The remote manager's end adds three wheel steps after the last reveal, and its dialog did not move.
- **Not covered natively:** track clicks and keyboard paging, 11 pt and other sizes and windows, macOS, native Wayland and fractional scale factors.

### Design review

A `design-reviewer` pass on the change and these frames (report received 02:26 UTC; it viewed every candidate state of all four lists in Midnight 13 pt, spot-checked the Porcelain and 18 pt variants and the base, and re-sampled the chooser's thumb colours, contrasts and widths at 13 pt in both palettes, and the Reflog frames' thumb colours): the change and the frames pass, with no violation of the task contract or `DESIGN.md`.
- **Resting thumb, 1.519:1 (Midnight) and 1.404:1 (Porcelain): acceptable.** `DESIGN.md` sets no 3:1 rule for scrollbar thumbs; the resting thumb is the `border` token, which it holds to 1.3:1, and is pixel for pixel the thumb of the approved Reflog frames (`docs/evidence/reflog-selection-and-scroll-cues/`); it reaches 8.42:1 and 6.37:1 hovered or pressed. Raising it would be an app-wide decision outside this task.
- **Spacing: acceptable.** The ring touches the track's derived edge without overlapping it, QA measured every ring's right side continuous in every frame, and the only visible gap, ring to thumb, is 6 px at rest and 4 px hovered; in the remote manager 3 px of surface separate the card border from the track.
- **The 19 px gutter: acceptable.** Row heights and density are unchanged; the chooser's labels stay put, and the Tags and Push to… labels move about 9.5 px to stay centred; a list that fits draws exactly as the base. The earlier truncation of the remote URL at 13 pt is the planned narrower card.
- **Crop edges: acceptable.** The titles cut at the top of the Tags and Remotes crops are the fixed crop box, not product clipping, and the `fits` crops reach onto History, which is what lets them compare 0 px against the base.
- **Return in the filtered Tags dialog:** a real keyboard defect of the dialogs, not of this change (inferred from the code; the base was not run) (see the findings above).
- **Recorded limitation:** `ScrollCue` sizes the gutter from the previous frame's overflow and redraws once when it changes, so a filter change inside an open dialog shows one frame at the old width (opening a dialog hides it behind the kit's 250 ms fade-in); by construction no frame paints a thumb over a row, and no transitional frame was captured.

Follow-ups for a planner, none in this task's scope: `DESIGN.md`'s new rule reads as covering every scrolling list of tab-stop rows (the contract's wording) while the worktree, profile and Reflog entry lists have no scrollbar yet, so name the four lists as its subject or list the exceptions; Return in the filtered Tags dialog, and possibly a focused branch-chooser row (`on_ok` runs `activate_first`) or a focused Edit… or Remove… in the remote manager, reaches the dialog's default action instead of the focused control (start from a GPUI test on the base); the remote card's truncated URL has no tooltip; Push to… keeps a fixed 160 px height at 18 pt; in Midnight a ghost row's hover is 1.058:1 against the dialog background; record the resting `border` and active `muted` thumb tokens in `DESIGN.md`.

## October 5 Blame and the conflict draft draw in the code font, and review line numbers fit the code size

Task `code-font-blame-conflicts-review-gutter`, attempt 1, rebased candidate `c017960` on base `a81c57a` (the run's accepted head and the candidate's parent). On the base, Blame's source lines drew in the fixed `mono()` family (the bundled DejaVu Sans Mono on Linux, even with the desktop monospace font on), the conflict editor's result draft set only a size and drew in the interface's proportional font, and the PR review's old and new line-number columns were a fixed 36 px at every code size. The candidate draws Blame's source (`blame_source`, `crates/app/src/blame.rs`) and the result draft (`resolution_textarea`, `crates/app/src/conflicts.rs`) through `.code_font(cx)`, the code family with its font features, so a ligature font shows the characters as typed; and it sizes the review's two columns for the file's widest line number (`line_number_width` and `patch_row`, `crates/app/src/github_view/review.rs`): ceil(widest digit advance × digit count), never under 36 px, where `appearance::code_digit_width` (`crates/app/src/appearance.rs`) measures the code family's widest digit advance at the current code size. Blame's source keeps its interface-relative size, and Compare's gutter is unchanged.

Automated, in `cargo test --locked -p gitturtle`, run by the candidate's gates:
- `blame::tests::blame_source_uses_the_code_font`: Blame's source cell, which takes `.code_font(cx)` in place of `mono()`, shapes its text in the theme's code family (`Desktop Mono` in the test) with `calt` disabled and `liga` set to 0.
- `conflicts::tests::resolution_textarea_uses_the_code_font`: the conflict editor's `resolution_textarea()` takes `.code_font(cx)`, so the result draft's text style carries the same family, `calt` off and `liga` 0, as the read-only sides' editors already did.
- `github_view::review::tests::line_numbers_fit_the_widest_number_at_every_code_size`: a patch row numbered 99,999 old and 12,345 new, its columns sized by `line_number_width` from `appearance::code_digit_width`. At the default 12 pt and at every size in `CODE_TEXT_RANGE` (10 to 24 pt) the old column is at least as wide as `99999` shaped in the code font, both columns are equally wide, the new column starts at or after the old column's end and the patch text after the new column's; the default column is at least 36 px and the 24 pt column wider than 36 px.

`DESIGN.md`'s ligature sentence ([Typography and density](../../DESIGN.md#typography-and-density)) now names every code-text site: diffs, editors, Blame, the conflict editor, Markdown code blocks, the PR review and the Settings sample; and it adds that the PR review sizes its line-number columns from the code font's digit width at the current code size, so the file's widest line number fits.

Native evidence, full tier: base and candidate in one session. Let `$EVIDENCE` be `/tmp/gitturtle-evidence`.
- **Builds:** base `a81c57a` (`a81c57a1ae41f893dc898e4659e54812968ecf56`, sha256 `e65cb2eba448db081e0a2a6fdc0f6bc5bbb14c447877ba49a26751c7d22fd3ac`, built 21:59–22:01 UTC) and candidate `c017960` (`c017960383818805800a3ff1cb8839bb2df92648`, sha256 `f4d300754a4ed01cfac9e4880ea9768d725c06f1df01f49d43e39279906623b6`, built 21:57–21:59 UTC). Both are release builds of clean trees, `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, rustc 1.99.0, each in its own fresh `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`; `qa.py identity` accepted the pair (22:02 UTC).
- **Host:** 2026-10-05, Ubuntu 26.04.1 LTS, GNOME 50.1, Linux X11 through XWayland `:0` at scale factor 1, `--input mutter`: every key, click and wheel step went through Mutter RemoteDesktop with X focus verified, never XTest. `qa.py display-check` found the desktop unlocked and no other QA process before and after each run; the unattended loop was idle. Windows 1000 × 680 for Blame and the review and 1000 × 900 for the conflict editor (a `resize` step: its sides, block actions and 160 px result draft need the height), interface 13 pt. AT-SPI (`org.a11y.Status IsEnabled`) was set for each launch, then restored and read back. The QA tooling ran from `main` at `266af80`.
- **The desktop monospace family:** every launch's store turns Settings' **Use the desktop's monospace font** on (`system_code_font: true`), and the spec seeds `FONTCONFIG_FILE=~/fontconfig-qa/fonts.conf` in each launch's HOME: an alias that prefers JetBrains Mono (`/usr/share/fonts/truetype/jetbrains-mono`, which has programming ligatures) for `monospace`, then includes the host's stock `/etc/fonts/fonts.conf` with its system cache, so `fc-match monospace` answers JetBrains Mono from a warm cache. The host's own `monospace` is DejaVu Sans Mono, the bundled family, which would have hidden the Blame change. This is not the owner's desktop configuration.

Its lines carry `->`, `>=`, `<=`, `&&`, `=>` and `!=`. Every launch left HEAD, status, index, refs, reflogs and worktrees unchanged.

Resize to 1000 × 900; "Go to Working Changes"; Home selects the conflicted file and the conflict editor opens its result draft; a click on the draft's first line and Home put the caret at the start of line 1, an x that does not depend on the font; AT-SPI must report the draft focused (`conflict`). Measured values (run 2; run 1 measured the same):
- **Clearance between the line-number columns and the patch text** (`clearance`, side right): each scan line starts at the column where a number's ink ends (the right edge of the spec's rect, set from the calibration) and counts the pixels within 6 units per channel of the surface, the tool's default tolerance, up to the first pixel further from it, the next ink on the row; the value is the minimum over the scan lines. The surface is the row's own colour sampled at window (309, y), y the row's middle (348 and 428 at 12 pt, 303 and 423 at 18 pt, 257 and 417 at 24 pt): (16, 21, 31) in Midnight and (246, 247, 252) in Porcelain. The scan lines are every pixel row of the digits at 12 and 18 pt (9 and 13 lines) and 16 of their 18 at 24 pt (all but y 253 and 262 on `20 22`, 413 and 422 on `22 25`), on the widest-number rows `20 22 fn refresh_review() {` and `22 25 }`. The values are identical in both builds and both palettes, and nothing overlaps (each had to be at least 1 px):

| Code size | Row | Old number's ink ends | Old → new number | New number's ink ends | New number → patch text |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 12 pt | `20 22` (y 344–352) | x 283 | 28 px | x 325 | 35 px |
| 12 pt | `22 25` (y 424–432) | x 283 | 28 px | x 325 | 37 px |
| 18 pt | `20 22` (y 297–309) | x 290 | 22 px | x 332 | 32 px |
| 18 pt | `22 25` (y 417–429) | x 290 | 22 px | x 331 | 34 px |
| 24 pt | `20 22` (y 249–266) | x 296 | 16 px | x 339 | 29 px |
| 24 pt | `22 25` (y 409–426) | x 297 | 15 px | x 338 | 33 px |

  The 18 pt `22 25` number-to-text value, 34 px, rests on that tolerance: x 332, beside the `5`, holds a one-unit fringe at y 425–426, (17, 22, 32) on (16, 21, 31) in Midnight and (245, 246, 251) on (246, 247, 252) in Porcelain, in both builds. The tool counts it as surface; counted as ink, it would give 33 px.

  The offline fixture's widest line number is 25. Two digits fit the 36 px minimum column at every code size Settings offers (2 × 14.4 px = 28.8 px in JetBrains Mono at 24 pt), so the candidate's columns are 36 px like the base's: the frames show no overlap and no narrowing, but no frame can show a number wider than 36 px. That case belongs to `github_view::review::tests::line_numbers_fit_the_widest_number_at_every_code_size` (a five-digit number at every code size up to 24 pt), which passed in the candidate's gates on `c017960`.
- **Masked base-versus-candidate compares** (`qa.py compare` inside the scenario):

  - `conflict-outside-draft-identical` (code 12 pt, bound 2) and `conflict-outside-draft-identical-code-18pt` (bound 1), crop `conflict` with the draft's interior masked and its focus ring and border compared: 2 px at 12 pt, (128, 214) and (571, 409), and 1 px at 18 pt, (128, 214); the ruled pixels and values above.

- **Accessibility (AT-SPI, every launch):** Blame's focused node is the list item "Line 1 · edcd746 · GitTurtle QA · pub fn within(line: u32, end: u32) -> bool {" (focused, selected); the conflict editor's is the entry "Manual conflict resolution result" (focused, editable) in all 8 conflict launches, which `draft-focused` requires; the review's is the list box "Pull request changed files".

In the caret probe the caret appeared 9–19 ms after Home. These are single observations per launch, not a timing claim.
- **The caret:** the draft's caret is 2 px wide at window x 65–66, y 660–673 (box `[65, 660, 67, 674]`), at the start of line 1, in the accent (117, 224, 187) in Midnight, in both builds and at both code sizes (caret probe). All 8 committed conflict crops, in both runs, show it in its off phase: the box matches the probe's caret-off frame in Midnight and holds no caret colour in Porcelain. It blinks every 500 ms, so a `qa.py recheck` of the conflict crops can differ inside that box alone; the compares mask it with the draft's interior.

`qa.py privacy scan --redacted --jobs 4 --templates .local/privacy/templates` (28 templates) found all 28 committed crops clean on their committed bytes in the candidate's tree (23:00 UTC), as did the run's own scan; the 28 files match run 2's `commit-manifest.json` by sha256. This entry and `scenario.json` were searched for personal strings (the owner's name, e-mail, user and host names, and home paths): none found (23:07 UTC). Each `scenario run` scanned its `commit/` with the same 28 templates (27.07 s in run 2, 27.04 s in run 1, no match in either), and each crop was viewed at full size: the crops show only the fixture's text, `GitTurtle QA`, the fixture's commit IDs and dates and the offline fixture's PR, with no repository path, account or host name.

Findings and limitations:
- **The one-unit pixels (ruled above):** the design reviewer rules them invisible and acceptable: not a defect and not a follow-up.
- **Found, present on the base too: Blame follows the interface size, not the code size.** Its source is `ui_text(11.)` in both builds, so each build's code 18 pt Blame crop is byte-identical to its 12 pt crop, and only the family changes with this task. Decision: a follow-up for a planner, low: draw Blame's source at `code_text()` and size its rows from the code line height, or say in `DESIGN.md` that it deliberately follows the interface size.
- **Found, present on the base too: the result draft's line pitch does not follow the code size.** At code 18 pt the draft keeps the 16 px pitch it has at 12 pt (its caret is 14 px tall at both), so its lines overlap, ascenders touching the descenders above; the base does the same in its proportional font. This goes against [`DESIGN.md`](../../DESIGN.md#typography-and-density)'s rules that row heights scale with their text and that code uses the native editor's measured line height. Decision: a follow-up for a planner: give the `Textarea` a line height relative to `code_text()` and size its fixed 160 px height from it.
- **Found, present on the base too: at code 18 pt each side pane cuts line 1 at its right edge.** [`DESIGN.md`](../../DESIGN.md#text-and-image-comparison) allows a cut for unwrapped lines ("Respect horizontal scrolling for unwrapped lines."); horizontal scrolling in the panes was not tested. The second gutter divider seen at 12 pt is also gone at 18 pt, in both builds. Decision: not a defect, no follow-up.
- **Wider numbers:** the review fixture's two-digit numbers (above) leave the wider-than-36 px case to the unit test. Optional evidence follow-up: a fixture file with three-digit line numbers would show the column widen natively (43.2 px at 24 pt).
- **Ligatures:** switched off, they cannot be shown against the base natively: the base draws Blame in DejaVu Sans Mono and the draft in the interface font, neither of which joins characters. The candidate's frames show `->`, `>=`, `<=`, `&&` and `!=` as typed in JetBrains Mono, which has those ligatures.
- **Local time:** the Blame detail line shows the commit time in the host's local zone, so a recheck on a host in another zone differs there.
- **Tooling:** a recipe cannot leave a conflicted merge, so the fixture was built outside the spec; `max_pixels` bounds a compare's pixel count, not where those pixels are; and a per-channel tolerance in `frames.compare` would remove the exact-count bounds.
- **Host limits:** Linux X11 through XWayland at scale factor 1; JetBrains Mono came through a QA `FONTCONFIG_FILE`, not the owner's desktop setting; no macOS, native Wayland or fractional scale factors; no code 24 pt for Blame or the draft (the criterion names 24 pt for the review only).
- **Not covered natively:** hover, scrolling Blame, typing in the draft, live GitHub, and Compare's gutter.

### Design review

Design review of the change and the frames (design-reviewer, 2026-10-05, report about 23:05 UTC): the change passes against `DESIGN.md`, and the frames pass as evidence. Blame's source, the result draft and the review rows draw through `.code_font(cx)`; the draft keeps its `aria_label`; the review's gutter is sized at `code_text()` with a 36 px floor; and the new [`DESIGN.md`](../../DESIGN.md#typography-and-density) sentence is accurate. The 28 crops, none a full window, cover every state, palette and size the criterion names; the draft is focused, holds text and shows its accent ring; and all 92 evaluations were as expected. Font check: a template match puts the base's Blame line closest to DejaVu Sans Mono at 11 px and the candidate's to JetBrains Mono (12 px ink height against 10), and `>=`, `<=`, `->` and `!=` draw as separate characters, so ligatures are off. Its rulings, recorded above under the findings: the one-unit pixels are invisible and acceptable, not a defect and not a follow-up; Blame following the interface size is a low follow-up for a planner and the draft's fixed line pitch a follow-up for a planner, both present on the base; the side panes cutting line 1 at 18 pt is not a defect and needs no follow-up. This entry's text was design-reviewed against the frames before it was committed.

## October 5 editor highlights keep text and syntax readable

Task `editor-highlights-keep-syntax-readable`, attempt 1, rebased candidate `3761488` on base `e5afb22` (the run's accepted head and the candidate's parent). Syntax colours were fitted to the editor's lines but not to the highlights drawn behind them, so on the base text and syntax could fall to 2.58:1 on a changed-word tint and 3.78:1 on the selection or a Find match. The candidate fits the highlights instead, as the owner decided on 2026-10-01: `EditorHighlights::fit` (`crates/app/src/appearance.rs`) derives the editor selection, Find's other and current match backgrounds and the added and removed changed-word tints from the palette, once in `Palette::apply`, never per frame, so text and every syntax colour read at least 4.5:1 on each (aim 4.75:1) and each stands at least 0.04 ΔEOK (Euclidean distance in OKLab) from every line it sits on. Syntax colours and the `selected` of rows and lists are unchanged. The rule and its measured extremes are in [`DESIGN.md`](../../DESIGN.md#semantic-palette-ownership) and the [themes specification](../development/themes/spec.md#diff-and-syntax-colors).

Automated, in `cargo test --locked -p gitturtle`, run by the candidate's gates:
- `appearance::tests::syntax_reads_on_every_editor_highlight` (the twenty built-ins and a faint-status custom theme), `appearance::omarchy::palette::tests::fixture_syntax_reads_on_every_editor_highlight` (the 24 embedded Omarchy fixtures as they map, Linux only) and `arbitrary_themes_always_map_to_a_readable_palette` (each of its 600 mapped themes) call `assert_editor_highlights_read`. It reads the colours the app applies (the configured theme's `selection`, every syntax field and the editor foreground) and requires each of them, and the unified patch's `added`, `removed` and `hunk`, at 4.5:1 or more on the selection, the Find match, the current match and both word tints, and each highlight at least 0.04 ΔEOK from every line it sits on. The specification records that all three fail on the base's colours.

Dumped and compared, for the `unchanged` criterion: a temporary test, byte-identical in both trees and never committed, dumped `Palette::configured_theme` in its dark and its light configuration for the twenty built-ins and the 24 embedded Omarchy fixtures as they map (88 configurations per build): the whole configured `HighlightTheme` (all 41 syntax styles with their colour, font style and weight, and the editor and status colours) with the editor foreground, and every row and list selection colour (the palette's `selected`, a hovered selected row and a selected control's fill, and the kit's `list_active`, `list_active_border`, `table_active`, `table_active_border`, `sidebar_accent`, `sidebar_accent_foreground`, `button_active`, `secondary_active` and `button_secondary_active` in both its colours and its tokens). Each build ran from a fresh clone of its commit in its own target directory (test profile), 2026-10-05, with no compiler crash. Syntax and row and list selection are byte-identical in all 88 configurations, 0 differences; only the text selection, dumped for information, changes, in 41 of the 44 palettes (Deep Sea, Graphite and Omarchy `last-horizon` keep their `selected`). The dumps and their description are in [`evidence/editor-highlights-keep-syntax-readable/unchanged/`](../evidence/editor-highlights-keep-syntax-readable/unchanged): `base.json`, `candidate.json` and `README.txt`, which names the fields, the palettes, the command and the comparison.

Native evidence, full tier: base and candidate in one session. Let `$EVIDENCE` be `/tmp/gitturtle-evidence`.
- **Builds:** base `e5afb22` (`e5afb228d53ebb4bf226cc8802c11ad7288ce737`, sha256 `cb4ba80ab60961ef677b88ed26aa6e82a029a9825a1eab38442f52b1ff942b0d`) and candidate `3761488` (`376148846a4431cfd2da88ac5cfd3f945a2a85b5`, sha256 `06ba761b949293ed6891541c59719ed9765adff424274dbae9065321e7a75fff`). Both are release builds of clean trees, `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, rustc 1.99.0, each built by `scripts/operator/build-release.sh` in its own fresh `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`; `qa.py identity` accepted the pair.
- **Host:** 2026-10-05, Ubuntu 26.04.1 LTS, GNOME 50.1, Linux X11 through XWayland `:0` at scale factor 1, `--input mutter`: every key, motion and button went through Mutter RemoteDesktop with X focus verified, never XTest. GNOME ScreenSaver reported `(false,)` and `qa.py display-check` reported the display clear (no GitTurtle process, driver or window, unlocked) before the first launch (18:36:12 UTC) and after the last (19:24:10 UTC). Windows 1200 × 800 at code 12 pt and 1700 × 900 at code 18 pt, so the Split view's longest line (`  let mut w: Span = fit(s.gap, "on", 42); // ups`, 48 columns) fits each side; interface text 13 pt throughout.
- **Fixture:** the spec's recipe at `$EVIDENCE/fixtures/editor-highlights-keep-syntax-readable/repo`, under the QA identity with no system or global configuration: `@0` `3af1f53` "Add the strip layout" adds `src/strip.rs`, a 13-line Rust file; HEAD `7b9ea14` "Fit the strip" on `main` replaces its lines 11 and 12 (`  let v: Grid = cut(s.pad, "off", 7); // down`, `  return v;`) with `  let mut w: Span = fit(s.gap, "on", 42); // ups` and `  w`. The recipe's `expect` pins both object IDs. The removed and added lines carry word-level changes in every syntax role (keyword `mut`/`return`, identifiers `v`/`w`, type `Grid`/`Span`, function `cut`/`fit`, field `pad`/`gap`, string `off`/`on`, number `7`/`42`, comment `down`/`ups`), and added line 11 repeats unchanged line 10 up to its comment, so the Find query `let mut w: Span = fit(s.gap, "on", 42); // up` matches twice, each match carrying every syntax colour. Its HEAD, status and index were unchanged by all 10 launches.

- **Route, the same input in both builds** (six guards per launch, all passed): History settles; a click on the HEAD row and Return open Compare in Diff (unified). A pointer drag in the focused patch editor selects from `+8,6 @@` on the hunk header to after `let mut w: Span =` on added line 11 (`unified-selection`). Ctrl+F and the query open Find, 1/2 (`unified-find`; the `type` step has no keysym for `;`, so the spec sends it as a key). Escape; Split; a click past the end of an unchanged line above the change in the After editor (line 1 at 12 pt, line 4 at 18 pt, where the split view opens scrolled), Ctrl+F and the same query (`split-find`). Escape; a drag in the focused Before editor from before `Span` on line 10 to after `let v: Grid` on removed line 11 (`split-selection`).

It exited 0, verdict pass: 10 launches with no warnings, 920 of 920 evaluations as expected (610 recorded only: the base's glyph contrasts, every ΔEOK fill and the base-against-candidate compares), and none of its 40 crops matched a privacy template.

Method. Contrast (`glyph_contrast`, 580 evaluations): one box per token inside each highlight, inset 1 px inside the token's own character cells (so it cuts only its own glyphs, never a neighbour's; `allow_edge`), and for the current match ending above its accent underline. The surface is the most frequent colour of the highlight's whole run on that line, and the ink colour is the peak: the highest-contrast ink colour backed by at least 2 ink pixels within 6 per channel, which is the solid glyph core. The candidate is held to 4.5:1 (`expect` true), the base only recorded. At code 12 pt thin glyphs (a lone `w`, `s`, `=`, `/`) may never show their colour unblended, so their peak is a lower bound: Midnight's comment colour `#A4B1C5` peaks at `#A2B0C3` in the 12 pt boxes and exactly at `#A4B1C5` at 18 pt. Distinctness (`fill`, 300 evaluations, recorded only): each highlight run's dominant colour and each line background's dominant colour, sampled in the same role's frames of the same launch; ΔEOK is the Euclidean distance between the two in OKLab (CSS Color 4's deltaEOK, Ottosson's matrices on 8-bit sRGB), computed from the colours `analysis.json` records, because no analysis kind measures it. The lines are the editor background (`canvas`, a text-free area right of the code), the added and removed line tints (the indent before `w` and `return`), and `panel`: Compare's editors draw no active line, so `panel` is sampled where the palette fills it beside the editors, the Find bar and the split headers. Each ΔEOK comes from a `fill` analysis in `scenario.json` named `g12-<capture>-<highlight>-vs-<line>` (`g18-` at code 18 pt), whose `frame` is the capture the highlight is sampled in; its reference is sampled in the same capture, except `unified-selection`'s panel and removed-line references, which come from `unified-find` (that capture has no Find bar, and the selection covers the removed lines). The highlight's colour is the most frequent colour of its region and the line's that of its reference region, so a reference box that reaches into a neighbouring row still records its line's colour, and `analysis.json` records both. Regions in window pixels, by capture. At code 12 pt (1200 × 800): `unified-selection`: selection `[147, 285, 349, 303]`, canvas `[560, 300, 900, 460]`, panel `[44, 205, 919, 252]` (in `unified-find`), added line `[154, 375, 169, 393]`, removed line `[154, 386, 169, 404]` (in `unified-find`), added word tint `[299, 357, 320, 375]`; `unified-find`: current match `[169, 350, 494, 364]`, canvas `[560, 300, 900, 460]`, panel `[44, 205, 919, 252]`, added line `[154, 422, 169, 440]`, removed line `[154, 386, 169, 404]`, other match `[169, 404, 494, 422]`, removed word tint `[169, 386, 234, 404]`; `split-find`: current match `[557, 452, 882, 466]`, canvas `[340, 300, 470, 380]`, panel `[482, 236, 919, 284]`, added line `[543, 488, 557, 506]`, removed line `[105, 487, 119, 505]`, other match `[557, 470, 882, 488]`, removed word tint `[119, 487, 184, 505]`; `split-selection`: selection `[199, 405, 444, 423]`, canvas `[340, 300, 470, 380]`, panel `[120, 207, 470, 233]`, added line `[543, 441, 557, 459]`, removed line `[105, 441, 119, 459]`, added word tint `[637, 423, 666, 441]`. Result: on the candidate, text and every syntax colour reach 4.5:1 or more on every highlight in all five variants, lowest 4.68:1 (Midnight 12 pt, comment on the added word tint, an anti-aliasing lower bound of the 4.75:1 measured at 18 pt) and otherwise 4.70:1 (Porcelain, comment `#4E5C79` on the added word tint `#BDE0CF`); every highlight stands at least 0.04 ΔEOK from every line, lowest 0.041 (Omarchy Tokyo Night's selection `#2C3147` from its removed line `#392835`, and Porcelain's current match `#BEDEFF` from its other matches `#D8E6FD`). On the base, every word tint misses 4.5:1 (lowest 2.80:1, Tokyo Night keyword `#AD8FE6` on the added word tint `#485A3E`), the selection and Find, on `selected`, reach it in these four palettes (lowest 4.67:1, Daylight and Tokyo Night), `selected` stands under 0.04 from the added line in Midnight (0.038), Daylight (0.017) and Porcelain (0.038, and from the removed line), and the current match is the other matches' colour (0.000).

`qa.py privacy scan --redacted --jobs 4 --templates .local/privacy/templates` (28 templates) found all 40 committed crops clean on their committed bytes in the candidate's tree (20:34 UTC), as did the run's own scan of the same bytes (26.9 s); every crop was also viewed at full size and shows only the fixture's text, line numbers, tints, the Find bar and the Before/After headers. This entry, `scenario.json` and the benchmark record were searched for personal strings (the owner's name, e-mail, user and host names, and home paths), as were the `unchanged` dumps and their description: none found (20:47 UTC).

Findings and limitations:
- **Found, design follow-up for a planner (within the stated measure, not a pass of it):** the fitting keeps each highlight apart from the lines it sits on, not from the other highlights, and on the candidate two pairs nearly match: Daylight's selection and other Find matches `#BEF6E4` against its added word tint `#C3F5DE` (ΔEOK 0.009), and Midnight's current match `#004836` against its added word tint `#114939` (0.011). On an added line, the end of a selection next to changed words at rest is hard to see: `candidate-daylight-13pt-1200x800-unified-selection.png`, added line 11, where the selected `let mut w: Span =` and the unselected `fit`, `gap` look alike; while the editor has focus the caret still marks the end, and without focus nothing does, which goes against the spirit of [DESIGN.md](../../DESIGN.md)'s "Selection remains visible after focus moves to another pane", a rule written for selected rows, so not a contract violation. On the base these pairs stood 0.106 to 0.113 apart, but the base's Daylight selection `#DAECE6` stood only 0.017 from the added line `#E2F1E9` itself, nearly invisible across whole added lines, which the candidate fixes (0.046). The Midnight pair is computed from the recorded fills, not shown in any frame: Find replaces the patch tints under its matches, and no frame puts a current match beside an unmatched changed word. Suggested rule: each editor highlight stands at least 0.04 ΔEOK from every other highlight that can touch it on the same line (the selection and Find's matches against both word tints), under the same readability floor.
- **Found, design follow-up for a planner:** changed-word emphasis is weaker on the candidate, and in two palettes it runs the other way. The base's word tints stood 0.116 to 0.149 ΔEOK from their lines, the candidate's 0.042 to 0.094. Daylight's removed word tint `#FCF6F7` is lighter than its removed line `#F9E5EB` (0.042), so the changed words read as gaps in the pink line, barely visible at 1x in `candidate-daylight-13pt-1200x800-unified-find.png` and in the Before editor of `candidate-daylight-13pt-1200x800-split-find.png`, lines 11 and 12, where the base marks them clearly; Omarchy Tokyo Night's added word tint `#233717` is darker than its added line `#2C3430` (0.048). Both meet the stated measure, and the specification documents the Daylight direction. The design reviewer tested its "only direction" claim: no tint that is not lighter than the line reaches 4.75:1 for Daylight's `#08745D` at 0.04 ΔEOK at any hue, and none reaches 4.5:1 within 15° of the line's OKLab hue; the nearest that do leave it, `#FDE0CB` (58°, 4.56:1) and, at a 4.6:1 floor, `#FFDDFF` (326°, 4.65:1), each exactly 0.040. It follows from the owner's decision to fit the highlights, not the syntax; the 0.04 floor rests on full-row selections (0.044), a weak basis for glyph-sized runs. Smallest fixes: a higher floor for word tints, or a lighter Daylight removed-line tint (a palette change, the owner's call).
- **Taste:** Daylight's current match `#93FDE6` is loud for a light theme.
- **The carets:** the focused editor's caret (selection frames) and the Find field's caret (Find frames) blink every 500 ms, so a committed crop can show either phase and a `qa.py recheck` can differ at the caret alone: 2 px wide at window x 291–292 (12 pt) and 405–406 (18 pt) on added line 11 in the unified selection, x 199–200 (12 pt) and 267–268 (18 pt) on removed line 11 in the split selection, and after `up` in the Find field. No glyph box or sample region touches a caret.
- **Tooling gaps:** `scenario` has no analysis kind for ΔEOK, so the distinctness values are computed outside the tooling from the fill colours it records, and the tooling records them without gating; and the `type` step has no keysym for `;` (`x11.CHAR_KEYSYMS`), so the query's semicolon is a `key` step.
- **Coverage:** the unified patch's current match carries only `text` (context line 10) and its other match only the added colour; the hunk and removed colours are measured on the selection, whose colour the candidate's other matches share (the same `#0B3E3E`, `#BEF6E4`, `#D8E6FD`, `#2C3147` in every frame), and the split frames carry every syntax colour on both matches. Compare's editors draw no active line, so `panel` is sampled from the Find bar and the split headers. Rows keep `selected`: the inspector's selected changed-file row was compared base against candidate as a window region in all 20 pairs, 0 px, with no committed crop of it. Not covered: macOS, native Wayland, fractional scale factors, the Before/After source tabs, the other built-in and Omarchy palettes, hover, and the theme editor's live preview.

### Performance

Same-session release builds, base `e5afb22` (sha256 `cb4ba80a…`, designated in writing at 19:29 UTC, before the first recorded launch) and candidate `3761488` (`06ba761b…`), every launch pinned with `taskset -c 0-7`, on this Linux host (i9-10900K, GNOME 50.1, XWayland `:0` at 137.95 Hz), with the host otherwise quiet (the loop idle; a read-only design review's image analysis ran pinned to CPUs 8, 9, 18 and 19, off the measured CPUs 0–7, from about 19:30 UTC until its report at about 19:34 UTC, before the first pilot launch). The fixture is the themes budget fixture (the demo repository plus 1,200 commits, History's first 1,000 loaded, a 2,000-line Split comparison retained). Record: [October 5 editor highlights theme apply](../benchmarks/2026-10-05-editor-highlights-keep-syntax-readable.md), with the raw samples in its `-builtin.csv`, `-omarchy.csv` and `-harness.csv`, the driver's log as `-driver-log.txt`, and the [JSON](../benchmarks/2026-10-05-editor-highlights-keep-syntax-readable.json). Eight unrecorded pilot launches came first (19:36 to 19:48 UTC); the 16 recorded launches ran from 19:49 to 20:20 UTC in an order fixed in writing at 19:48:53 UTC.
- **Built-in switches:** picker clicks across all twenty built-in palettes, launches in the order B C C B B C C B, 320 recorded switches per build (16 per palette). `gitturtle.theme_apply_frame_ms` on the candidate: p50 3.860 ms, p95 7.079 ms, max 7.897 ms (base 3.699 / 7.055 / 7.684), within the [themes budget](../development/themes/spec.md#performance) of median ≤ 8 ms and p95 ≤ 16 ms. That metric ends at a next-frame callback on this display's 7.249 ms tick, so it lies between the handler's own cost and that cost plus one tick and cannot see a change this size.
- **Omarchy switches:** Tokyo Night and Catppuccin Latte alternating, launches in the order C B B C C B B C, 160 per build. `gitturtle.omarchy_apply_frame_ms` on the candidate: 3.788 / 7.109 / 7.853 ms (base 3.643 / 7.150 / 7.858).
- **UI-thread CPU to the new theme's frame,** the number that moves with code: +0.136 ms at p50 over the built-ins (95% interval −0.017 to +0.242) and +0.288 ms on Omarchy (+0.202 to +0.356; the per-launch medians do not overlap). Per palette it tracks the harness below (slope 1.01, r = 0.75): One Light adds 0.612 ms in the app and 0.542 ms in the harness.
- **Release harness of `Palette::apply`'s work:** `EditorHighlights::fit` adds 46 µs (Deep Sea) to 577 µs (Sandstone) per application at p50 (p95 up to 628 µs, first call up to 647 µs); light palettes cost the most, and two custom drafts whose text fails the readability rule about 0.49 ms. The theme editor applies a draft up to once per frame while a colour picker is dragged, so on the measured palettes and the two stress drafts that path gains at most 0.58 ms at p50, 0.63 ms at p95 and 0.65 ms on a first call (the p95 and first-call figures are the whole application's, a conservative bound), under 9% of this display's 7.249 ms frame; the search is bounded by construction (at most 257 lightness steps and 20 chroma rows of 65 steps per highlight), but no draft was built to maximise it, so this is a measured bound, not a worst case; it was not timed natively (`gitturtle.theme_edit_frame_ms` is open).
- **Cache state:** warm page cache, cold shader cache and a fresh store and HOME in every launch, with 4 unrecorded warm-up switches per launch.
- **Verdict:** within the theme-apply budget; the added cost is real and small. Not measured: the theme editor's live preview, the specification's two custom themes in the click cycle, presentation and GPU time, native Wayland and macOS.

### Design review

Design review of the frames (design-reviewer, 2026-10-05, crops opened 19:30–19:32 UTC, report about 19:34 UTC): the change passes and the frames pass. Text and every syntax colour read at 4.5:1 or more on the selection, both Find highlights and both word tints in all five variants; Find's matches keep a selection tint and the current match its accent underline in every candidate Find crop ([DESIGN.md](../../DESIGN.md)'s Find rule); rows keep `selected` (the 20 inspector compares at 0 px); and `DESIGN.md` states the new rule as the specification does. The frames cover every state, palette and size the criterion names, the measures were taken from them, and every crop is an editor region, not a full-window project hub frame. The reviewer opened all 40 crops and re-sampled the fills (Daylight's selection `#BEF6E4`, added word tint `#C3F5DE`, added line `#E2F1E9`, removed word tint `#FCF6F7` on its line `#F9E5EB`, current match `#93FDE6`; Midnight's selection `#0B3E3E`, current match `#004836`, added word tint `#114939`; Tokyo Night's selection `#2C3147` and removed line `#392835`; Porcelain's removed word tint `#EFD4DC` on `#F8E5ED`), four ink pixels and the ΔEOK values 0.009, 0.011, 0.042, 0.044, 0.046 and, on the base, 0.106, 0.113 and 0.116, all matching. Its rulings, recorded above under the findings: the near-match of the selection and Find with the added word tint is a follow-up for a planner, not a blocker, because the stated measure is each highlight against its line and every frame meets it (lowest 0.041), and the candidate fixes the larger defect of the base's Daylight selection on added lines; the weaker and, in Daylight and Tokyo Night, reversed word tints are a second follow-up; Daylight's loud current match is taste. The draft's sample regions were not tied to their captures, corrected above. This entry's text was design-reviewed against the frames before it was committed.

## October 5 patch decorations count toward the retained-bytes bounds

Task `decorations-in-retained-bounds`, attempt 1, rebased candidate `c9e1222` on base `7323988`. Compare's unified patch, the Stashes inspector and the rewritten-series review build decoration lists beside their `PatchPresentation`, but no retained-bytes figure counted them. Compare's tab figure, `tab_retained_bytes()` in `crates/app/src/repository_tabs.rs`, now adds `patch_decoration` through `PatchDecorations::retained_bytes`; the presentation was already counted inside the content's bytes. That figure is checked against the 512 MiB `MAX_RETAINED_BYTES` bound when switching tabs. The Stashes inspector (`recovery::StashBrowser`) and the rewritten-series review (`rewrite_review::SeriesBrowser`) used to drop their decorations handle; they now build their patch editor with `text::editor_with_decorations`, keep the handle until the preview is cleared, and expose `preview_retained_bytes()`. Neither modal inspector has a bound of its own (tab switching is blocked while a dialog is open), so, following the nearest-owner rule, the figure sits on each browser and no global budget was added. `docs/architecture.md`, `crates/app/docs/navigation-and-refresh.md` and `crates/app/docs/content-and-layout.md` name decorations among what each bound counts.

Automated, in `cargo test --locked -p gitturtle`, run by the candidate's gates:
- `repository_tabs::tests::compare_patch_decorations_count_toward_the_retained_tabs_bound` builds a real decorated patch editor on the active tab, checks that the tab figure grows by exactly the decorations' bytes and that the content accounts for at least the presentation's, and sets the warm tab so the undecorated figure sits at the bound: the switch is refused with the 512 MiB error while decorated and succeeds without the decorations. Without the new accounting line it fails.
- `recovery::tests::stash_patch_preview_counts_its_decorations_and_presentation` and `rewrite_review::tests::series_patch_preview_counts_its_decorations_and_presentation` build the real browsers and check each preview's figure; they cannot compile on the base, whose browsers have no decorations field.
- The PR review's Source stays counted: `github_view::review` tests, including `source_patch_takes_the_decorations_its_file_preparation_made`, pass.

Native evidence, full tier: base and candidate in one session. Let `$EVIDENCE` be `/tmp/gitturtle-evidence`.

The unattended loop was idle in an evidence wait. Window 1000 × 800, so the Stashes dialog (window height minus 230) shows its whole hunk; Midnight, 13 pt.

### Performance

The [October 5 large-patch record](../benchmarks/2026-10-05-decorations-in-retained-bounds.md) measured opening a large text patch in Compare and in the Stashes inspector on a disposable fixture whose two files' patches are 26,672 lines (1.83 MB) each. The base `7323988` (sha256 `4d142847…`) and the candidate `c9e1222` (`a79234e1…`) were designated in writing by the coordinator at 16:33 UTC, before the first recorded launch, and checked with `qa.py identity`. Every app launch ran under `taskset -c 0-7` (the driver on CPUs 8–9), Midnight 13 pt at 1480 × 800, with fresh HOME and XDG directories (shader and app caches cold, page cache warm), in orders fixed in writing before each block: Compare 16 launches (8 per build, 40 recorded opens each, 320 per build), the Stashes inspector 12 launches (240 opens per build), and a supplementary Compare block of 8 launches (160 opens per build), 16:44 to 17:20 UTC. Input: Mutter RemoteDesktop Down and Up between the two large files every 700 ms. Timing on X11: key-down D-Bus send to the first X `DamageNotify` after the app's `file_preview_frame_ms` trace line (the Stashes inspector, which prints no trace, to its last damage), and the UI thread's CPU from `schedstat`. Base against candidate, p50 / p95 / max in ms: Compare key to patch frame 25.54 / 26.62 / 44.5 against 25.55 / 27.21 / 119.5 (Δp50 +0.010 [−0.17, +0.19]); Compare UI CPU 20.49 / 21.49 / 23.9 against 20.49 / 22.40 / 35.2 (Δp50 +0.004 [−0.12, +0.16]); supplementary Compare UI CPU 20.39 / 21.16 / 22.2 against 20.30 / 20.92 / 33.7 (Δp50 −0.096 [−0.23, +0.01]); Stashes key to last frame 26.93 / 29.60 / 55.5 against 26.95 / 28.05 / 41.5 (+0.019 [−0.21, +0.31]); Stashes UI CPU 22.63 / 24.11 / 28.4 against 22.64 / 23.38 / 28.1 (+0.017 [−0.13, +0.19]). Cold opens took about 302–315 ms in both builds; frame counts per open matched and no idle window drew a frame. The main Compare block's UI-CPU p95 (+0.91 ms [+0.01, +2.05]) clusters in three candidate launches, is not significant at the launch level (p = 0.12) and reverses in the supplementary block; four isolated 33–35 ms opens, all on the candidate, fit contention on their physical core, and three of them came while the coordinator's tooling work ran on the SMT siblings of the app's CPUs (the record gives the account). Verdict: no regression beyond noise. The driver and fixture generator land beside the record in their own commit. The rewritten-series review's open was not timed; its change is the Stashes inspector's.

### Design review

Design review (design-reviewer, 2026-10-05), the escalation the criterion asks for after the failed run: PASS. The failed run's pixel is per-launch rasterization variance: the base executable, unchanged in every run, drew it both ways, only that pixel moved, and nothing in the diff reaches the status bar or glyph drawing (the hint text is drawn by `crates/app/src/views.rs`, which the diff does not touch; `tab_retained_bytes()` is read only by the tab-switch admission check; the new `decoration` fields are written and cleared and read only by the test-only `preview_retained_bytes()`; and `text::editor` was already `editor_with_decorations(…).0`, so the editor is built the same way). The six committed crops show each state's unified patch with its decorations (tinted headers, removed and added rows with tinted gutter numbers, word-level highlights), each pair byte-identical. Follow-ups, not caused by this change: the whole-window comparisons could tolerate one unit per channel or mask the hint's anti-aliased edges so that this variance does not fail a run again (QA tooling); in both builds the added and removed row tints cover only the text, not the full row width (taste).

## October 5 History's columns stay put after navigation is hidden and shown

Task `history-columns-steady-after-toggle`, attempt 1, candidate `bc4726e` on base `d72b4c4`. History measured its width in `on_prepaint` and took a new width only when it differed by more than 1 px (`crates/app/src/views.rs`), and Commit message (Subject) takes the leftover width. At a fractional scale factor, hiding and showing navigation could therefore leave a width up to 0.4 px stale, which moved Subject's right edge and every column after it. The candidate keeps the measured width exactly and notifies only when it changes, so the same window width always lays the columns out the same way, and an unchanged width lays out nothing. The scope toolbar's compact threshold now uses the exact width too, so it switches to its labelled row 1 px of window width sooner.

Automated, in `cargo test --locked -p gitturtle`, run by the candidate's gates:
- `views::tests::history_columns_return_after_toggling_navigation` (new) presses Ctrl+B (Cmd+B on macOS) twice at windows of 1000, 1480 and 1398 px and scale factors 2, 1, 1.25 and 1.5. It checks that navigation is hidden, then shown, and that every column header's edges and History's width equal those before the toggle. With `main`'s 1 px tolerance restored it fails at 1398 px and scale 1.25, where History measured 937.6 px before and 938.0 px after and the edges moved 0.8 px.
- `views::tests::history_lays_out_again_only_for_a_new_width` (new): at scale 2 and 1.25 and 1398 and 1213 px, a resize takes a new width, and four forced redraws at the unchanged width take none, through a test-only counter of the widths History takes.
- Column headers carry debug selectors (`history-column-<n>`, no-ops in release builds) for those tests.
- The scope toolbar's exact-width threshold is covered by the existing `views::tests::scope_toolbar_grows_in_order_as_history_widens`, which still passes; no native frame reaches that threshold, since both builds draw the labelled toolbar at 1600 px.

Native evidence, full tier: base and candidate in one session. Let `$EVIDENCE` be `/tmp/gitturtle-evidence`.
- **Builds:** base `d72b4c4`, the run's accepted head and the candidate's parent (sha256 `caae2b3857c764d81fdabbb44d89683968cb56ffdbdad0d6c977fd01c3984fba`), and candidate `bc4726e` (sha256 `3931e0b86f7fb40af1ce110b0274c868c469ec9841976ad99ff7f62fac857424`). Both are release builds of clean trees, `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, rustc 1.99.0, each built by `scripts/operator/build-release.sh` in its own fresh `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, deleted after the copy; `qa.py identity` accepted the pair.
- **Host:** 2026-10-05, Ubuntu 26.04.1 LTS, GNOME 50.1, Linux X11 through XWayland `:0`, `--input mutter`: every key went through Mutter RemoteDesktop with X focus verified, never XTest. `qa.py display-check` found the desktop unlocked and no other QA process before and after. The unattended loop was paused.
- **Fixture:** the spec's recipe at `$EVIDENCE/fixtures/history-columns-steady-after-toggle/repo`, 19 commits under the QA identity (HEAD `700dec8`), unchanged by every launch. Each launch had its own HOME and XDG directories and a generated store with Follow system off.
- **Window:** 1600 × 800, wider than History's house size of 1480 × 800. At 1480 the 18 pt inspector leaves History narrower than the 910 px its columns need, so a horizontal scrollbar appears and Commit message takes no leftover, which would hide the very edge this change keeps still. At 1600 the leftover is 106 px at 13 pt and 33 px at 18 pt (History 1016 and 943 px wide; Commit message 426 and 353 px between its dividers).
- **Route, the same input in both builds:** the launch settles on History and is captured (`rest`); Escape (Clear Search, with an empty search) puts the window in keyboard mode, so every compared frame draws the list's focus ring, and the window is captured (`before`); Ctrl+B, with a guard that navigation changed (`hidden`); Ctrl+B again, with a guard that navigation is back (`after`). Ctrl+B first changed the window 44 to 69 ms after the key in 23 of 24 presses (median 50.5 ms, n = 24, from the 12 launches' flow logs); one press, the base's second Ctrl+B at Midnight 18 pt, took 186 ms. Every capture settled about 1.2 s after its step.
- **Scale factor 1, the scenario:** `qa.py scenario run` of `docs/evidence/history-columns-steady-after-toggle/scenario.json` (sha256 `289dcd3e4624de074220d56deca1d4d113e3f3601eb496cffdeae36451149f58`) with both builds into `$EVIDENCE/runs/history-columns-steady-after-toggle`, 2026-10-05 from 15:04:54 to 15:08:24 UTC. It exited 0: 8 launches with no warnings, 16 guards passed, 32 of 32 analyses as expected (28 recorded only), and none of its 20 crops matched a privacy template. An earlier run with the same steps (`…-superseded-e82c949`, deleted) produced byte-identical crops; it was rerun only to correct the spec's limitations prose.
- **Scale factor 1.25, outside the scenario:** a scenario cannot set the scale factor, so these frames were taken with `qa.py launch --scale 1.25 --size 2003x1000 --display :0 --input mutter --for-commit`, the scenario's steps without its guards, and stores generated from the spec's variants (sha256 equal to those the scenario seeds), Midnight at 13 and 18 pt, 2026-10-05 08:36:29 to 08:37:41 UTC, into `$EVIDENCE/runs/history-columns-steady-after-toggle-1.25x/w2003/`. 2003 × 1000 device px is 1602.4 × 800 logical, where History measures 1,271 device px (1016.8 logical) at 13 pt and 1,179 (943.2) at 18 pt, a fractional logical width. The GPUI test's 1398 logical px cannot be reproduced at 1.25 with a whole device width: at 1748 px History is 887.2 logical, below the 910 px the columns need, so Commit message takes no leftover and nothing can shift. The crops were cut with the tracked `native_qa.evidence.cut`, the scenario's own encoder, at the boxes below scaled to device pixels.

Frames in [`evidence/history-columns-steady-after-toggle/`](../evidence/history-columns-steady-after-toggle), the scenario's 20 crops; the spec beside them is the run's byte for byte. This contradicts `DESIGN.md`'s History column contract, that the header and rows use the same computed layout and horizontal offset. It is identical on base and candidate (`hidden-same-on-both-*`: 0 px) and the same at 18 pt and in Porcelain, X11 did not redraw it (a re-grab 4 s later was identical), so this change did not cause it; it is committed as the only record of the defect, for its own task.
